Auriculocondylar Syndrome

Mendelian MONDO:0000107 Pathograph 49 Show in embeddings browser ear malformation craniofacial malformation syndrome

Auriculocondylar syndrome is a congenital craniofacial disorder characterized by variable mandibular and condylar hypoplasia, question-mark ears and associated oral or temporomandibular abnormalities. Severe presentations can compromise breathing and feeding. Dominant GNAI3 or PLCB4 variants, recessive PLCB4 or EDN1 alleles, and a reported HDAC9-TWIST1 regulatory duplication act through distinct molecular routes. Development, hearing and extra-craniofacial findings are variable; incomplete penetrance occurs.

Inheritance

2
Autosomal dominant HP:0000006
Many reported PLCB4 and GNAI3 families show autosomal dominant inheritance, often with variable expressivity and incomplete penetrance.
Autosomal dominant inheritance Expressivity: VARIABLE
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:28328130 SUPPORT BACKGROUND Human Clinical
"Auriculocondylar syndrome, mainly characterized by micrognathia, small mandibular condyle, and question mark ears, is a rare disease segregating in an autosomal dominant pattern in the majority of the families reported in the literature."
This cohort review directly supports autosomal dominant inheritance as the most common reported pattern.
PMID:38618928 SUPPORT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Human Clinical
"As with other studies examining familial cases of ARCND2, incomplete penetrance and variable expressivity were observed within different families' heterozygous mutations in PLCB4 gene."
The 2024 review supports variable expressivity and incomplete penetrance in PLCB4-related families.
Autosomal recessive HP:0000007
Recessive auriculocondylar syndrome has been reported for EDN1 variants and for biallelic PLCB4 loss-of-function lesions.
Autosomal recessive inheritance
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:24268655 SUPPORT PRIMARY RESULT Human Clinical
"By whole-exome sequencing (WES), we identified a homozygous substitution in a furin cleavage site of the EDN1 proprotein in ACS-affected siblings born to consanguineous parents."
This directly supports recessive EDN1-related auriculocondylar syndrome.
PMID:23315542 SUPPORT PRIMARY RESULT Human Clinical
"CONCLUSIONS: These findings indicate that ACS is not only genetically heterogeneous but also an autosomal dominant or recessive condition according to the nature of the PLCB4 gene lesion."
This directly supports recessive inheritance for some PLCB4 lesions.

Subtypes

5
ARCND1 (GNAI3-related) MONDO:0011234
GNAI3 hgnc:4387 HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee (hgnc) Relation: this subtype is caused by variation in this gene This subtype is caused by variation in GNAI3 (hgnc:4387). hgnc:4387 is a gene from the HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee.
Usually autosomal dominant auriculocondylar syndrome caused by heterozygous GNAI3 variants affecting GDP/GTP-binding motifs and EDN1 pathway signaling.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:38618928 SUPPORT BACKGROUND Human Clinical
"Four subtypes of ARCND have been described so far, that is, ARCND1 (OMIM # 602483), ARCND2 (ARCND2A, OMIM # 614669; ARCND2B, OMIM # 620458), ARCND3 (OMIM # 615706), and ARCND4 (OMIM # 620457)."
This identifies ARCND1 and its OMIM identifier.
PMID:22560091 SUPPORT PRIMARY RESULT Human Clinical
"In both kindreds, the p.Gly40Arg variant was inherited; in one case (S011) it was inherited from an affected mother (S012-P), and in the other (S008), from an unaffected father (S009-P), documenting incomplete penetrance."
Two molecularly characterized families establish dominant inheritance with incomplete penetrance.
ARCND2A (PLCB4-related, autosomal dominant) MONDO:0013845
PLCB4 hgnc:9059 HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee (hgnc) Relation: this subtype is caused by variation in this gene This subtype is caused by variation in PLCB4 (hgnc:9059). hgnc:9059 is a gene from the HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee.
Autosomal dominant PLCB4-related auriculocondylar syndrome, typically caused by heterozygous missense variants. Note on the binding: MONDO has not adopted OMIM's ARCND2A/2B relabel, so MONDO:0013845 is still plain "auriculocondylar syndrome 2" with no "2A" synonym and no inheritance axiom. It is bound here on the strength of its OMIM:614669 xref, which matches this row exactly. It and MONDO:0957544 (bound to the ARCND2B row below) both point at PLCB4 and are distinguished only by OMIM entry, not by a dominant/recessive assertion in MONDO.

ARCND3 (EDN1-related) MONDO:0014312

EDN1-related ACS is established with recessive alleles. Dominant EDN1 variants also cause isolated question-mark ears, and a heterozygous start-loss case had mild mandibular involvement. Residual ligand activity is a proposed explanation for allelic differences, not a direct patient-allele assay.

ARCND4 (TWIST1 regulatory duplication) MONDO:0957543
TWIST1 hgnc:12428 HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee (hgnc) Relation: this subtype is caused by variation in this gene This subtype is caused by variation in TWIST1 (hgnc:12428). hgnc:12428 is a gene from the HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee.
A 430-kb tandem duplication within HDAC9, outside TWIST1 coding sequence, segregated in one family. It encompasses candidate TWIST1 regulatory elements and is associated with increased TWIST1 and HDAC9 expression in patient-derived neural-crest cells; the causal contributions of these changes remain unresolved.

Discussions and Knowledge Gaps

3
Which regulatory and cellular changes mediate the HDAC9-region duplication phenotype?
KNOWLEDGE GAP arcnd_regulatory_mediation
Attached to
pathophysiology#HDAC9-TWIST1 Regulatory Duplication pathophysiology#Increased Neural Crest TWIST1 Expression
Segregation, nonpatient Capture-C and patient-cell expression findings support a regulatory locus. They do not isolate TWIST1 from HDAC9 or establish that the scratch and osteogenic readouts cause the human jaw phenotype. No serial TWIST1-to-migration-to-osteogenesis causal chain is asserted.
Show evidence (4 references)
PMID:34750192 SUPPORT PRIMARY RESULT Human Clinical
"The duplication spanned 430 302 bp (NC_000007.14:g.18437239_18867540dup) telomeric to TWIST1, covering most of the HDAC9 gene."
A segregating tandem duplication in one four-generation family defines the regulatory locus; TWIST1 coding sequence is not duplicated.
PMID:34750192 SUPPORT INDIRECT PRIMARY RESULT In Vitro
"However, an increase in HDAC9 (3.15-fold, unpaired t-test p=0.009) and TWIST1 (2.03-fold, unpaired t-test p=0.03) mRNA was observed in the ARCND-iNCC (figure 2A, B) compared with controls (unpaired t-test)."
Three affected-donor iPSC-derived neural-crest cultures show increased transcripts relative to three controls; no isogenic correction isolates the causal mediator.
PMID:34750192 SUPPORT INDIRECT PRIMARY RESULT In Vitro
"Although no significant difference in cell cycle distribution was detected between patient and control cells, a significant decrease in migratory capacity of ARCND iNCCs was observed compared with controls (4.3-fold decrease, Student’s t-test, p=0.0009) (figure 2C–E)."
A 24-hour scratch assay measures reduced wound closure in the patient-derived cultures; it does not measure migration into human pharyngeal arches.

How do variant mechanism and species affect the craniofacial phenotype?
HUMAN MODEL MISMATCH arcnd_species_and_alleles
Attached to
pathophysiology#Reduced EDNRA-Dependent Phospholipase C Activity pathophysiology#Abnormal Mandibular Skeletal Morphogenesis
Human recessive PLCB4 loss-of-function disease coexists with reports of normal skull morphology in Plcb4-null mice. The Arg621His founder experiment tested homozygous and knock-in/indel genotypes, not a simple heterozygote. Fish plcb3 mutants and broad endothelin-pathway knockouts establish developmental requirements without making every model equivalent to a human ACS allele.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:35284927 SUPPORT PRIMARY RESULT Model Organism
"Of the 69 embryos harvested at E18.5, we identified three embryos harboring at least one knock-in allele; one was homozygous for the knock-in allele (Plcb4KI/KI) and the other two were compound heterozygotes harboring the knock-in allele and an insertion/deletion (indel) allele generated by..."
The actual founders comprise one homozygote and two knock-in/indel embryos, not simple heterozygotes.
PMID:27072656 SUPPORT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Model Organism
"knockout mice lacking PLCβ4 do not display a craniofacial phenotype"
The mechanistic study reviews an established species/model discrepancy.
Which extra-craniofacial findings are causally attributable to individual ACS genotypes?
KNOWLEDGE GAP arcnd_extra_craniofacial_scope
Attached to
phenotypes#Central apnea phenotypes#Cerebral arteriovenous malformation
Central apnea may persist after correction of mechanical obstruction. Small series report vascular, rib, developmental and genital findings, but do not establish precise population risks or the cellular intermediates. These observations are retained without forcing every finding through mandibular homeosis.
Show evidence (2 references)

The clinical synthesis distinguishes central apnea from the structural obstructive component.

Pathophysiology

18
Heterozygous GNAI3 Variants
Heterozygous pathogenic GNAI3 variants cluster in nucleotide-binding regions. De novo and inherited alleles occur, with variable expression and some clinically unaffected carriers. Functional conclusions below apply to the five tested missense variants rather than every subsequently reported allele.
GNAI3 hgnc:4387 HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee (hgnc) Relation: this pathophysiological event involves this gene This pathophysiological event involves GNAI3 (hgnc:4387). hgnc:4387 is a gene from the HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee.
Animal models
GNAI3-variant expression in Xenopus embryos Xenopus laevis perturbs Introduces the disease-associated protein substitutions into an embryonic expression assay.
Downstream
Impaired GNAI3 GTP Binding Patient-associated substitutions impair nucleotide-dependent activation in the tested proteins.
Question mark ear This molecular subtype is associated with the characteristic pinna malformation; the detailed cellular route to lobe-helix separation is unresolved.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:22560091 SUPPORT PRIMARY RESULT Human Clinical
"In both kindreds, the p.Gly40Arg variant was inherited; in one case (S011) it was inherited from an affected mother (S012-P), and in the other (S008), from an unaffected father (S009-P), documenting incomplete penetrance."
Two molecularly characterized families establish dominant inheritance with incomplete penetrance.
Impaired GNAI3 GTP Binding
Purified GNAI3 S47R retains GDP binding but fails to bind GTP efficiently. G40R, G45V, T48N and N269Y also show impaired GTP-dependent protease protection after expression in HEK293T cells. This replaces the earlier structure-based gain-of-function prediction; it does not establish generalized loss of GDP binding.
GNAI3 hgnc:4387 HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee (hgnc) Relation: this pathophysiological event involves this gene This pathophysiological event involves GNAI3 (hgnc:4387). hgnc:4387 is a gene from the HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee.
Experimental models
Purified GNAI3 S47R nucleotide-binding assays OTHER Biochemical assays distinguish GDP retention from defective GTP binding.
Downstream
Increased Mutant GNAI3 Receptor Association Failure to complete nucleotide-dependent activation is proposed to favor nonproductive receptor complexes. Increased association is measured, but the kinetic trapping mechanism is inferred.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:27072656 SUPPORT PRIMARY RESULT In Vitro
"Together, these results demonstrate that Gαi3 S47R retains the capability to bind GDP but fails to bind GTP, rendering the G protein unable to switch into an active conformation."
Purified S47R directly retains GDP but loses efficient GTP binding; other variants were assessed with cell-expressed limited-proteolysis assays.
Increased Mutant GNAI3 Receptor Association
The five tested mutant GNAI3 proteins associate more strongly with EDNRA in co-immunoprecipitation and with Ric-8A in yeast assays. Sustained nonproductive receptor occupancy is the proposed explanation for interference with another G-protein class; binding kinetics were not directly measured.
GNAI3 hgnc:4387 HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee (hgnc) Relation: this pathophysiological event involves this gene This pathophysiological event involves GNAI3 (hgnc:4387). hgnc:4387 is a gene from the HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee.
Experimental models
GNAI3-variant signaling in HEK293T cells CELL LINE Co-immunoprecipitation shows greater mutant association with EDNRA.
Downstream
Reduced EDNRA-Gq Signaling Nonproductive mutant-GNAI3 association is proposed to limit receptor availability for Gq activation. The horizontal inhibitory effect is experimentally observed; receptor competition is its mechanistic interpretation.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:27072656 SUPPORT PRIMARY RESULT In Vitro
"We found that all 5 ACS-associated Gαi3 mutants displayed increased binding (3- to 5-fold) to myc-tagged ETAR compared to Gαi3 WT (Fig. 5D)."
Co-immunoprecipitation measures increased receptor association, not a directly measured receptor residence time or equilibrium affinity.
Reduced EDNRA-Gq Signaling
EDNRA preferentially activates Gq in the tested heterologous system. Disease-associated GNAI3 proteins suppress that response while leaving the weak Gi3 response largely unchanged under the same conditions. Normal GNAI3 is therefore not represented as an obligatory positive intermediate between EDNRA and PLCB4.
Experimental models
GNAI3-variant signaling in HEK293T cells CELL LINE BRET measures reduced Gq activation after ET-1 stimulation.
Downstream
Reduced EDNRA-Dependent Phospholipase C Activity Reduced receptor-coupled Gq activation diminishes phospholipase-C-dependent second-messenger generation.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:27072656 SUPPORT PRIMARY RESULT In Vitro
"We found that expression of any of the five ACS-associated Gαi3 mutants decreased Gq activation upon ET-1 stimulation ~65 to 85% (Fig. 6D)."
HEK293T BRET assays show reduced Gq activation when mutant Gi3 is coexpressed at a substoichiometric ratio.
PLCB4 Disease Alleles
PLCB4 disease alleles include dominant catalytic-domain missense variants and recessive biallelic loss-of-function lesions. Haploinsufficiency alone does not explain the tested dominant variants. The neonatal Ser643Phe report classified its variant as likely pathogenic and supplied structural prediction, not a catalytic assay.
PLCB4 hgnc:9059 HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee (hgnc) Relation: this pathophysiological event involves this gene This pathophysiological event involves PLCB4 (hgnc:9059). hgnc:9059 is a gene from the HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee.
Downstream
Reduced EDNRA-Dependent Phospholipase C Activity Tested dominant missense alleles interfere with EDNRA-dependent PLC activity. Recessive loss-of-function alleles provide a distinct route to deficient PLCB4 function.
Question mark ear This molecular subtype is associated with the characteristic pinna malformation; the detailed cellular route to lobe-helix separation is unresolved.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:22560091 SUPPORT PRIMARY RESULT Human Clinical
"We identified two additional novel PLCB4 missense mutations at c.1862G>A (M001) and c.1948A>C (M002)"
The primary report identifies segregating dominant PLCB4 variants.
PMID:24268655 SUPPORT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Human Clinical
"In addition, two cases have been ascribed to homozygous or compound-heterozygous loss-of-function mutations in PLCB4."
Summarizes previously established biallelic PLCB4 cases.
Reduced EDNRA-Dependent Phospholipase C Activity
EDN1-stimulated DAG-sensor translocation and BRET are reduced by tested PLCB4 variants in HEK293T cells. Introducing Pro838Ala at the Gq-interaction interface in cis with Arg621His attenuates its dominant interference. These are cell-based pathway readouts, not purified-enzyme kinetics; substrate trapping and altered GAP activity remain untested explanations.

Experimental models
PLCB4-variant DAG and transcriptional reporter assays CELL LINE Patient-associated substitutions attenuate pathway output and interface disruption reduces interference.
Downstream
Reduced DLX5 and DLX6 Expression PLCB4/GNAI3 pathway disruption is associated with reduced downstream DLX expression in patient osteoblasts. The intervening embryonic transcriptional route is not resolved by these cultures.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:35284927 SUPPORT PRIMARY RESULT In Vitro
"These results illustrate that PLCB4 p.Arg621His interferes with PLCB activity even when it is expressed at a sub-stoichiometric level relative to wild-type PLCB4, exemplifying a characteristic of a dominant-negative protein."
DAG-sensor BRET detects dominant interference by Arg621His; three additional tested missense variants also attenuated the response.
EDN1 Disease Alleles
Biallelic EDN1 substitutions were identified in recessive ACS families. Other EDN1 alleles cause dominant isolated question-mark ears, and a later start-loss heterozygote had mild mandibular involvement. Phenotype and inheritance depend on the allele; measured residual ligand activity is not available for the original patient substitutions.
EDN1 hgnc:3176 HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee (hgnc) Relation: this pathophysiological event involves this gene This pathophysiological event involves EDN1 (hgnc:3176). hgnc:3176 is a gene from the HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee.
Downstream
Reduced Bioactive EDN1 Availability Variants near processing sites or within the peptide are predicted to reduce effective ligand availability or activity. The original study did not directly assay cleavage or receptor activation by these patient alleles.
Question mark ear This molecular subtype is associated with the characteristic pinna malformation; the detailed cellular route to lobe-helix separation is unresolved.

PMID:24268655 SUPPORT PRIMARY RESULT Human Clinical
"In family F1, the unaffected parents and child were heterozygous for the mutation, whereas the three affected siblings were homozygous, indicating that the allele encoding the p.Lys91Glu substitution behaves in a recessive manner."
Segregation supports recessive inheritance of Lys91Glu in this family.
Reduced Bioactive EDN1 Availability
Reduced effective EDN1 ligand is the proposed functional consequence of disease alleles. Lys91Glu alters a furin recognition motif and Pro77His lies near the ECE cleavage region. Functional evidence from different substitutions supports this model but cannot quantify residual activity of these ACS alleles.
EDN1 hgnc:3176 HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee (hgnc) Relation: this pathophysiological event involves this gene This pathophysiological event involves EDN1 (hgnc:3176). hgnc:3176 is a gene from the HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee.
Downstream
Reduced EDNRA-Gq Signaling Reduced active ligand is expected to diminish EDNRA activation and its Gq signal in arch mesenchyme.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:24268655 SUPPORT INDIRECT PRIMARY RESULT Human Clinical
"The different mode of inheritance for the F1 and S1 mutant EDN1 alleles compared to the F2 and F3 alleles might be explained by differences in the amount of residual functional protein in each case."
The authors explicitly propose residual functional ligand as an explanation; patient-allele processing was not directly measured.
Reduced DLX5 and DLX6 Expression
DLX5 and DLX6 transcripts were reduced in four postnatal ACS mandibular-osteoblast cultures relative to calvarial controls. Mouse developmental experiments support the importance of the EDNRA-DLX program, but the patient assay is not a direct measurement of fetal neural-crest transcription.

ACS patient mandibular osteoblast cultures PRIMARY CELL CULTURE RT-qPCR detects lower DLX transcript abundance in the patient cultures.

Disrupted Mandibular Neural Crest Identity Reduced DLX program activity compromises mandibular specification, based on developmental genetic models. The full sequence is not traced in human embryos.

PMID:22560091 SUPPORT PRIMARY RESULT In Vitro
"Using quantitative, real-time RT-PCR of steady-state mRNA, we identified a 6-fold reduction of DLX5 (p < 0.005; probands versus controls) and an 8-fold reduction of DLX6 expression (p < 0.001) in mandibular osteoblasts from selective probands relative to 17 randomly selected calvarial..."
Four ACS mandibular-osteoblast cultures were compared with 17 calvarial-osteoblast controls; anatomical source and developmental stage limit interpretation.
Disrupted Mandibular Neural Crest Identity
EDNRA-DLX disruption changes the positional identity of mandibular neural-crest-derived mesenchyme in animal models. Human skeletal morphology is consistent with partial mandibular-to-maxillary homeosis; it does not prove that every clinical feature arises through this transformation.
neural crest cell CL:0011012 Cell Ontology (CL) Relation: this pathophysiological event involves this cell type This pathophysiological event involves neural crest cell (CL:0011012). CL:0011012 is a cell type from the Cell Ontology.
Animal models
Endothelin-pathway knockout mice Mouse perturbs Pathway-gene deletion changes mandibular positional identity.
Zebrafish edn1 and plcb3 developmental mutants Zebrafish perturbs Endothelin-pathway perturbation alters lower-jaw patterning.
Downstream
Abnormal Mandibular Skeletal Morphogenesis Altered mandibular identity produces abnormal skeletal differentiation and patterning in developmental models, with corresponding morphology in selected human cases.
Middle-Ear Ossicular Malformation Arch-identity signaling is required for ossicular development in mice. Applying this route to human fusion is an inference across species.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:24123988 SUPPORT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Model Organism
"Disruption of this signaling pathway in both mouse and zebrafish results in loss of identity of neural crest cells of the mandibular portion of the first pharyngeal arch and the subsequent repatterning of these cells, leading to homeosis of lower jaw structures into more maxillary-like structures."
Genetic developmental models support altered identity rather than a universal neural-crest migration deficit.
HDAC9-TWIST1 Regulatory Duplication
A 430-kb tandem duplication within HDAC9 segregated with ACS in one Brazilian family. It includes candidate TWIST1 regulatory elements while leaving the TWIST1 coding region outside the duplication. Some short HDAC9 isoforms are wholly duplicated, whereas longer catalytic isoforms cross a breakpoint.
TWIST1 hgnc:12428 HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee (hgnc) Relation: this pathophysiological event involves this gene This pathophysiological event involves TWIST1 (hgnc:12428). hgnc:12428 is a gene from the HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee. HDAC9 hgnc:14065 HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee (hgnc) Relation: this pathophysiological event involves this gene This pathophysiological event involves HDAC9 (hgnc:14065). hgnc:14065 is a gene from the HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee.
Downstream
Increased Neural Crest TWIST1 Expression The duplication is associated with increased TWIST1 expression in patient-derived neural-crest cells. Regulatory contact data support, but do not directly prove, enhancer-mediated upregulation.
Increased Neural Crest HDAC9 Expression Patient-derived neural-crest cells also show increased HDAC9 transcripts. The contribution of this change to craniofacial development remains unresolved.
Reduced Migration of Patient-Derived Neural Crest Cells Duplication-bearing cultures show reduced scratch closure; the respective roles of TWIST1, HDAC9 and donor background were not separated by rescue experiments.
Impaired Osteogenic Differentiation in Patient-Derived Cells Duplication-bearing mesenchymal derivatives show impaired osteogenic readouts. This parallel observation does not establish that impaired migration causes impaired osteogenesis.
Question mark ear This molecular subtype is associated with the characteristic pinna malformation; the detailed cellular route to lobe-helix separation is unresolved.
Micrognathia The duplication segregates with variable jaw hypoplasia. The cultured migration and osteogenic phenotypes have not been shown to mediate this clinical outcome.

A segregating tandem duplication in one four-generation family defines the regulatory locus; TWIST1 coding sequence is not duplicated.
PMID:34750192 SUPPORT PRIMARY RESULT Human Clinical
"the majority presented with question mark ears (8 of 10), microstomia (8 of 10) and micrognathia (6 of 10)"
The duplication family has variable core craniofacial findings.
Increased Neural Crest TWIST1 Expression
TWIST1 mRNA is increased in patient-derived neural-crest cultures, but not detectably different in undifferentiated iPSCs or during mesenchymal osteogenic differentiation. No TWIST1-specific correction or overexpression experiment in this study proves mediation of the cellular defects.

Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:34750192 SUPPORT INDIRECT PRIMARY RESULT In Vitro
"However, an increase in HDAC9 (3.15-fold, unpaired t-test p=0.009) and TWIST1 (2.03-fold, unpaired t-test p=0.03) mRNA was observed in the ARCND-iNCC (figure 2A, B) compared with controls (unpaired t-test)."
Three affected-donor iPSC-derived neural-crest cultures show increased transcripts relative to three controls; no isogenic correction isolates the causal mediator.
Increased Neural Crest HDAC9 Expression
HDAC9 mRNA is increased in duplication-bearing neural-crest cultures. Transcript abundance does not establish increased catalytic HDAC9 activity, and a contribution independent of TWIST1 cannot be excluded.

Three affected-donor iPSC-derived neural-crest cultures show increased transcripts relative to three controls; no isogenic correction isolates the causal mediator.
Reduced Migration of Patient-Derived Neural Crest Cells
Patient-derived iNCC cultures have reduced 24-hour scratch closure without a detected cell-cycle distribution change. Positional identity and developmental stage of these induced cells require further characterization; reduced embryonic neural-crest colonization remains a hypothesis.
neural crest cell CL:0011012 Cell Ontology (CL) Relation: this pathophysiological event involves this cell type This pathophysiological event involves neural crest cell (CL:0011012). CL:0011012 is a cell type from the Cell Ontology.

Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:34750192 SUPPORT INDIRECT PRIMARY RESULT In Vitro
"Although no significant difference in cell cycle distribution was detected between patient and control cells, a significant decrease in migratory capacity of ARCND iNCCs was observed compared with controls (4.3-fold decrease, Student’s t-test, p=0.0009) (figure 2C–E)."
A 24-hour scratch assay measures reduced wound closure in the patient-derived cultures; it does not measure migration into human pharyngeal arches.
Impaired Osteogenic Differentiation in Patient-Derived Cells
Patient-derived neural-crest mesenchymal cells show reduced day-9 alkaline-phosphatase activity and a smaller mineralization readout. These findings support delayed or impaired osteogenic differentiation in culture, not demonstrated causation of the human mandibular phenotype.
neural-crest-derived mesenchymal cell CL:0008019 Cell Ontology (CL) Relation: this pathophysiological event involves this cell type This pathophysiological event involves neural-crest-derived mesenchymal cell, annotated with mesenchymal cell (CL:0008019). "Our data showed that during osteogenic differentiation, ALP enzymatic activity was significantly diminished in ARCND-nMSCs after 9 days of osteoinduction (decrease of 20.3-fold, paired t-test p=0.029; figure 3A, B)."
Patient-derived neural-crest mesenchymal cells show reduced alkaline-phosphatase activity during osteogenic induction.
Abnormal Mandibular Skeletal Morphogenesis
Abnormal mandibular skeletal development produces a short or dysplastic ramus, hypoplastic condyle and variable temporomandibular joint abnormalities. Selected severe cases have maxilla-like skeletal features. Associated oral, pinna and middle-ear findings need not all be mediated by mandibular homeosis.
Animal models
Plcb4 Arg621His edited mouse founders Mouse recapitulates Embryonic skeletal imaging and staining demonstrate abnormal proximal mandibular development.
Downstream
Micrognathia The structural jaw-development defect manifests as micrognathia in affected individuals.
Mandibular condyle hypoplasia The structural jaw-development defect manifests as mandibular condyle hypoplasia in affected individuals.
Temporomandibular joint ankylosis The structural jaw-development defect manifests as temporomandibular joint ankylosis in affected individuals.
Upper-Airway Narrowing A small malformed jaw and associated oral structures can narrow the upper airway, particularly with posterior tongue displacement.
Feeding difficulties Structural oral and mandibular abnormalities can impair feeding; oral-motor contributions vary.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:35284927 SUPPORT PRIMARY RESULT Model Organism
"the proximal mandible of Plcb4Indel/KI and Plcb4KI/KI embryos was disorganized, with absent or hypoplastic mandibular processes"
E18.5 edited mouse founders show proximal mandibular skeletal abnormalities; the tested genotypes are not simple wild-type/knock-in heterozygotes.
PMID:22560091 SUPPORT PRIMARY RESULT Human Clinical
"In each case, mandibular ankylosis was progressive, of variable severity, and characterized by inconsistent fusion to the medial and lateral pterygoid plates."
The selected severe clinical cases document variable progressive ankylosis.
Upper-Airway Narrowing
Mandibular and tongue abnormalities can narrow the oropharyngeal airway. This mechanical branch explains obstructive respiratory complications in selected patients; persistent central apnea requires separate assessment.
Downstream
Respiratory distress Severe upper-airway compromise can produce neonatal respiratory distress.
Obstructive sleep apnea Mechanical upper-airway compromise can contribute to obstructive apnea during sleep.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:38618928 SUPPORT PRIMARY RESULT Human Clinical
"Computed tomography (CT) revealed a narrowed oropharynx airway and absent condylar fossae, shortened mandibular rami, and obtuse mandibular angles (Figure 2)."
CT documents oropharyngeal narrowing in the severely affected neonate.
Middle-Ear Ossicular Malformation
Middle-ear malformation can include congenital-appearing malleoincudal fusion. Histology supports an abnormal joint-development process in one adolescent; coexisting cholesteatoma can contribute to worsening hearing.
Downstream
Conductive hearing impairment Ossicular fusion can impede sound conduction; the reported case also had cholesteatoma, so the contribution of each lesion was not experimentally isolated.
Show evidence (1 reference)
url:https://lab.research.sickkids.ca/wp-content/uploads/sites/22/2015/09/Propst_et_al_Ossicular-Fusion-and-cholesteatoma-in-Auriculo-Condylar-Syndrome_2013.pdf SUPPORT PRIMARY RESULT Human Clinical
"the malleoincudal joint appeared to be partially ankylosed laterally ."
Temporal-bone CT demonstrates the structural ossicular abnormality in one case.

Genetic Associations

5
GNAI3 pathogenic variants (Causative)
Gene: GNAI3 hgnc:4387 HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee (hgnc) Relation: this disease-associated gene is this gene This disease-associated gene is GNAI3 (hgnc:4387). hgnc:4387 is a gene from the HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee. relationship_type: CAUSATIVE
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:22560091 SUPPORT PRIMARY RESULT Human Clinical
"In both kindreds, the p.Gly40Arg variant was inherited; in one case (S011) it was inherited from an affected mother (S012-P), and in the other (S008), from an unaffected father (S009-P), documenting incomplete penetrance."
Two molecularly characterized families establish dominant inheritance with incomplete penetrance.
PMID:27072656 SUPPORT PRIMARY RESULT In Vitro
"Together, these results demonstrate that Gαi3 S47R retains the capability to bind GDP but fails to bind GTP, rendering the G protein unable to switch into an active conformation."
Purified S47R directly retains GDP but loses efficient GTP binding; other variants were assessed with cell-expressed limited-proteolysis assays.
PLCB4 pathogenic variants (Causative)
Gene: PLCB4 hgnc:9059 HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee (hgnc) Relation: this disease-associated gene is this gene This disease-associated gene is PLCB4 (hgnc:9059). hgnc:9059 is a gene from the HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee. relationship_type: CAUSATIVE
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:35284927 SUPPORT PRIMARY RESULT In Vitro
"These results illustrate that PLCB4 p.Arg621His interferes with PLCB activity even when it is expressed at a sub-stoichiometric level relative to wild-type PLCB4, exemplifying a characteristic of a dominant-negative protein."
DAG-sensor BRET detects dominant interference by Arg621His; three additional tested missense variants also attenuated the response.
Biallelic PLCB4 disease alleles (Causative)
Gene: PLCB4 hgnc:9059 HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee (hgnc) Relation: this disease-associated gene is this gene This disease-associated gene is PLCB4 (hgnc:9059). hgnc:9059 is a gene from the HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee. relationship_type: CAUSATIVE
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:23315542 SUPPORT PRIMARY RESULT Human Clinical
"The consanguineous parents of the patient with a homozygous PLCB4 deletion each harboured the heterozygous deletion, but did not present the ACS phenotype, further suggesting that ACS is not caused by PLCB4 haploinsufficiency."
Segregation demonstrates a recessive deletion mechanism in one family.
EDN1 pathogenic variants (Causative)
Gene: EDN1 hgnc:3176 HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee (hgnc) Relation: this disease-associated gene is this gene This disease-associated gene is EDN1 (hgnc:3176). hgnc:3176 is a gene from the HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee. relationship_type: CAUSATIVE
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:24268655 SUPPORT PRIMARY RESULT Human Clinical
"In family F1, the unaffected parents and child were heterozygous for the mutation, whereas the three affected siblings were homozygous, indicating that the allele encoding the p.Lys91Glu substitution behaves in a recessive manner."
Segregation supports recessive inheritance of Lys91Glu in this family.
PMID:24268655 SUPPORT INDIRECT PRIMARY RESULT Human Clinical
"The different mode of inheritance for the F1 and S1 mutant EDN1 alleles compared to the F2 and F3 alleles might be explained by differences in the amount of residual functional protein in each case."
The authors explicitly propose residual functional ligand as an explanation; patient-allele processing was not directly measured.
TWIST1 regulatory duplication (Causative)
Gene: TWIST1 hgnc:12428 HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee (hgnc) Relation: this disease-associated gene is this gene This disease-associated gene is TWIST1 (hgnc:12428). hgnc:12428 is a gene from the HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee. relationship_type: CAUSATIVE
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:34750192 SUPPORT PRIMARY RESULT Human Clinical
"The duplication spanned 430 302 bp (NC_000007.14:g.18437239_18867540dup) telomeric to TWIST1, covering most of the HDAC9 gene."
A segregating tandem duplication in one four-generation family defines the regulatory locus; TWIST1 coding sequence is not duplicated.
PMID:34750192 SUPPORT INDIRECT PRIMARY RESULT In Vitro
"However, an increase in HDAC9 (3.15-fold, unpaired t-test p=0.009) and TWIST1 (2.03-fold, unpaired t-test p=0.03) mRNA was observed in the ARCND-iNCC (figure 2A, B) compared with controls (unpaired t-test)."
Three affected-donor iPSC-derived neural-crest cultures show increased transcripts relative to three controls; no isogenic correction isolates the causal mediator.

Medical Actions

10
Individualized airway support
Action: Respiratory TherapyNCI Thesaurus (NCIT) Relation: this treatment is this clinical intervention This treatment is Respiratory Therapy (NCIT:C15322). NCIT:C15322 is a clinical intervention from the NCI Thesaurus. NCIT:C15322
Platform: Other
Severe neonatal obstruction may require specialist airway stabilization, including tracheostomy. Positive-pressure support may be needed for sleep-disordered breathing. Assessment must distinguish central from obstructive components and consider associated airway lesions.
Mechanism Target:
BYPASSES Upper-Airway Narrowing — Tracheostomy can bypass an obstructed upper airway; its use is individualized.

Diagnosis

4
Clinical and craniofacial imaging assessment
Recognize the variable combination of question-mark ears, micrognathia and condylar or temporomandibular abnormalities. Examine oral structures, hearing and airway function. Imaging can characterize ramus, condyle, joint and temporal-bone anatomy where clinically indicated; absence of a complete triad does not exclude disease.
Show evidence (3 references)
url:https://pure.rug.nl/ws/files/217832848/Human_Mutation_2022_Vegas_Further_delineation_of_auriculocondylar_syndrome_based_on_14_novel_cases_and_reassessment.pdf SUPPORT PRIMARY RESULT Human Clinical
"Question mark ear Total 37/38 97%"
The table counts assessed members of a selected 39-person ACS/IQME cohort.
PMID:38618928 SUPPORT PRIMARY RESULT Human Clinical
"Computed tomography (CT) revealed a narrowed oropharynx airway and absent condylar fossae, shortened mandibular rami, and obtuse mandibular angles (Figure 2)."
CT documents oropharyngeal narrowing in the severely affected neonate.
url:https://lab.research.sickkids.ca/wp-content/uploads/sites/22/2015/09/Propst_et_al_Ossicular-Fusion-and-cholesteatoma-in-Auriculo-Condylar-Syndrome_2013.pdf SUPPORT PRIMARY RESULT Human Clinical
"the malleoincudal joint appeared to be partially ankylosed laterally ."
Temporal-bone CT demonstrates the structural ossicular abnormality in one case.
Molecular and structural-variant testing
Interpret targeted sequencing or exome/genome results together with phenotype and segregation. Established genes include GNAI3, PLCB4 and EDN1. Copy-number and regulatory-region analysis is relevant in unresolved cases because the reported HDAC9-TWIST1 duplication lies outside TWIST1 coding sequence. A VUS alone does not establish the diagnosis, and selected-case yields are not general diagnostic sensitivity.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:28328130 SUPPORT PRIMARY RESULT Human Clinical
"We found novel pathogenic variants in PLCB4 only in two of three index patients with typical Auriculocondylar syndrome."
Two positive typical index cases in an 11-person mixed-phenotype series; not an unselected sensitivity estimate.
PMID:34750192 SUPPORT PRIMARY RESULT Human Clinical
"The duplication spanned 430 302 bp (NC_000007.14:g.18437239_18867540dup) telomeric to TWIST1, covering most of the HDAC9 gene."
A segregating tandem duplication in one four-generation family defines the regulatory locus; TWIST1 coding sequence is not duplicated.
Prenatal assessment
Severe fetal micrognathia with polyhydramnios can prompt detailed craniofacial imaging and molecular evaluation. These findings are not specific for ACS, and question-mark ears may not be identified prenatally.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:34789173 SUPPORT PRIMARY RESULT Human Clinical
"Severe micrognathia and mandibular hypoplasia accompanied by polyhydramnios are prenatal indicators of ACS."
The reported fetus provides a possible diagnostic clue rather than a validated screening rule.
Audiologic and sleep evaluation
Audiograms help distinguish conductive, sensorineural and mixed or unclassified hearing loss. Sleep studies can distinguish obstructive and central components and inform follow-up after structural treatment.
Show evidence (2 references)
url:https://pure.rug.nl/ws/files/217832848/Human_Mutation_2022_Vegas_Further_delineation_of_auriculocondylar_syndrome_based_on_14_novel_cases_and_reassessment.pdf SUPPORT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Other
"Attention needs to be paid to middle and inner ear function by way of an audiogram in all patients with ACS to evaluate for hearing loss."
Case-series authors recommend audiologic assessment.
url:https://pure.rug.nl/ws/files/217832848/Human_Mutation_2022_Vegas_Further_delineation_of_auriculocondylar_syndrome_based_on_14_novel_cases_and_reassessment.pdf SUPPORT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Human Clinical
"Central sleep apnea has previously been reported in patients with ACS."
The clinical synthesis distinguishes central apnea from the structural obstructive component.
📈

Progression

2
Prenatal and neonatal presentation
Craniofacial malformations are congenital and severe mandibular hypoplasia can be recognized prenatally, sometimes with polyhydramnios. Severe neonates may need early airway and feeding intervention; question-mark ears can be absent.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:34789173 SUPPORT PRIMARY RESULT Human Clinical
"Severe micrognathia and mandibular hypoplasia accompanied by polyhydramnios are prenatal indicators of ACS."
The prenatal case supports early detectability, not diagnostic specificity.
Childhood and adult course
Joint restriction, sleep-disordered breathing, hearing, feeding, dental and articulation problems may require staged follow-up. Development is often normal but is not uniformly preserved. Structural treatment does not ensure resolution of central apnea. Adult corrective surgery has favorable reported outcomes in individual cases, not a comparative natural-history estimate.
Show evidence (3 references)
PMID:22560091 SUPPORT PRIMARY RESULT Human Clinical
"In each case, mandibular ankylosis was progressive, of variable severity, and characterized by inconsistent fusion to the medial and lateral pterygoid plates."
Direct clinical characterization of the selected severe cases.
url:https://pure.rug.nl/ws/files/217832848/Human_Mutation_2022_Vegas_Further_delineation_of_auriculocondylar_syndrome_based_on_14_novel_cases_and_reassessment.pdf SUPPORT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Human Clinical
"Central sleep apnea has previously been reported in patients with ACS."
The clinical synthesis distinguishes central apnea from the structural obstructive component.
url:https://pure.rug.nl/ws/files/217832848/Human_Mutation_2022_Vegas_Further_delineation_of_auriculocondylar_syndrome_based_on_14_novel_cases_and_reassessment.pdf SUPPORT PRIMARY RESULT Human Clinical

Prevalence

1
Reported global estimate; sampling frame unspecified
Point Prevalence <1 in 1,000,000
The 2024 case report repeats this rarity estimate from prior literature, rather than measuring a population rate. The separate statement that fewer than100 cases had been reported is a literature count, not its denominator; geographic and time-specific ascertainment are not provided.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:39014351 SUPPORT BACKGROUND Human Clinical
"an extremely rare autosomal dominant or recessive condition with a prevalence of less than 1 in 1,000,000"
The source reports a broad prevalence band as background, not a new epidemiologic measurement.
🔀

Differential Diagnoses

6

Conditions with similar clinical presentations that must be differentiated from Auriculocondylar Syndrome:

Pierre Robin sequence-plus
Overlapping Features Pierre Robin sequence and related craniofacial disorders overlap through micrognathia, glossoptosis and palatal abnormalities. The ear pattern, condylar anatomy, other findings and molecular testing distinguish the spectrum; absence of question-mark ears alone does not exclude ACS.
Distinguishing Features
  • Question mark ears
  • Mandibular condyle hypoplasia
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:28328130 SUPPORT PRIMARY RESULT Human Clinical
"In order to address these questions, we searched for alterations in PLCB4, GNAI3, and EDN1 in patients with typical Auriculocondylar syndrome (n = 3), Pierre Robin sequence-plus (n = 3), micrognathia with additional craniofacial malformations (n = 4), or non-specific auricular dysplasia (n = 1),..."
This supports Pierre Robin sequence-plus as part of the clinical differential or spectrum considered in testing.
MEF2C-related neurodevelopmental disorder
Overlapping Features Question-mark ears accompanied by severe intellectual disability or epilepsy can indicate a different molecular disorder and merit broader evaluation.
Distinguishing Features
  • Severe encephalopathy or epilepsy outside the usual ACS presentation
Show evidence (1 reference)
url:https://pure.rug.nl/ws/files/217832848/Human_Mutation_2022_Vegas_Further_delineation_of_auriculocondylar_syndrome_based_on_14_novel_cases_and_reassessment.pdf SUPPORT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Human Clinical
"Recent reports have highlighted that heterozygous myocyte enhancer factor 2C (MEF2C; MIM# 600662) loss ‐of‐function mutations should be con- sidered as a possible cause of QME associated with severe intellectual deficiency and/or epilepsy (Gordon et al., 2018;"
The cohort discussion identifies a distinct molecular differential; this does not reclassify all ACS-associated developmental delay.
Oculo-auriculo-vertebral spectrum
Overlapping Features This craniofacial spectrum is a named source of diagnostic confusion. Assess the ear, mandibular-condylar and broader craniofacial pattern; the report identifies overlap but does not provide a validated diagnostic classifier.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:39014351 SUPPORT BACKGROUND Human Clinical
"ARCND can be misdiagnosed as other diseases, including craniomaxillofacial deformities such as the oculo-auriculo-vertebral spectrum ... Treacher Collins syndrome ... mandibulofacial dysostosis ... Guion-Almeida type, and Meier-Gorlin syndrome"
The paper explicitly names this differential as background; it does not measure the sensitivity or specificity of distinguishing signs.
Treacher Collins syndrome
Overlapping Features Treacher Collins syndrome is a named differential for the mandibulofacial presentation. Question-mark ear morphology and condylar findings can direct ARCND testing, but neither a single ear sign nor mandibular hypoplasia alone establishes the molecular diagnosis.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:39014351 SUPPORT BACKGROUND Human Clinical
"ARCND can be misdiagnosed as other diseases, including craniomaxillofacial deformities such as the oculo-auriculo-vertebral spectrum ... Treacher Collins syndrome ... mandibulofacial dysostosis ... Guion-Almeida type, and Meier-Gorlin syndrome"
The paper explicitly names this differential as background; it does not measure the sensitivity or specificity of distinguishing signs.
Mandibulofacial dysostosis, Guion-Almeida type
Overlapping Features The cited background lists this named mandibulofacial dysostosis among potential misdiagnoses. Broader clinical assessment and phenotype-guided molecular testing are appropriate when the craniofacial pattern is atypical for ARCND.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:39014351 SUPPORT BACKGROUND Human Clinical
"ARCND can be misdiagnosed as other diseases, including craniomaxillofacial deformities such as the oculo-auriculo-vertebral spectrum ... Treacher Collins syndrome ... mandibulofacial dysostosis ... Guion-Almeida type, and Meier-Gorlin syndrome"
The paper explicitly names this differential as background; it does not measure the sensitivity or specificity of distinguishing signs.
Meier-Gorlin syndrome
Overlapping Features The case-report background names Meier-Gorlin syndrome as a potential diagnostic alternative. Interpret auricular and mandibular findings in the complete clinical context instead of treating ear morphology alone as diagnostic.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:39014351 SUPPORT BACKGROUND Human Clinical
"ARCND can be misdiagnosed as other diseases, including craniomaxillofacial deformities such as the oculo-auriculo-vertebral spectrum ... Treacher Collins syndrome ... mandibulofacial dysostosis ... Guion-Almeida type, and Meier-Gorlin syndrome"
The paper explicitly names this differential as background; it does not measure the sensitivity or specificity of distinguishing signs.

Related Datasets

1
Next Generation Mendelian Genetics: Auriculocondylar Syndrome (ACS) dbgap:phs000437
This project is part of an ongoing project to identify the molecular genetic basis of rare craniofacial disorders. This specific project involved the collection of DNA samples from four probands with severe manifestations of auriculocondylar syndrome and their parents. Detailed clinical phenotypic data is available on each proband. The sole purpose of this research is to identify the molecular cause(s) of auriculochondylar syndrome.
The dbGaP accession and disease-specific title were verified against the live repository on 2026-09-21. The project describes four severe ACS probands and their parents; it is a gene-discovery collection, not a representative phenotype-frequency cohort.
🧫

Experimental Models

6
Purified GNAI3 S47R nucleotide-binding assays OTHER
Recombinant wild-type and S47R GNAI3 were compared by nucleotide-binding, GTPase, HPLC and limited-proteolysis assays. S47R retains GDP but has profoundly impaired GTP binding and activation.
Cell source
Recombinant proteins expressed in Escherichia coli
Publication
PMID:27072656
Pathograph links
Impaired GNAI3 GTP Binding measures Biochemical assays distinguish GDP retention from defective GTP binding.
Limitations: Direct purified-protein evidence is limited to S47R.
Readouts
GDP and GTP binding
GDP is retained while GTP binding is impaired.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:27072656 SUPPORT PRIMARY RESULT In Vitro
"Together, these results demonstrate that Gαi3 S47R retains the capability to bind GDP but fails to bind GTP, rendering the G protein unable to switch into an active conformation."
Purified S47R directly retains GDP but loses efficient GTP binding; other variants were assessed with cell-expressed limited-proteolysis assays.

The other four variants could not be purified in sufficient soluble quantity; their activation defects were tested after mammalian expression. Poor recovery is not a direct protein half-life measurement.

GNAI3-variant signaling in HEK293T cells CELL_LINE
Five ACS-associated GNAI3 variants were studied in transfected HEK293T cells using protease protection, receptor co-immunoprecipitation and BRET. ET-1-induced Gq activation was reduced at an 8:1 Gq-to-Gi3 expression ratio; the weak Gi3 response was not comparably inhibited.
Cell source
Transfected human embryonic kidney 293T cell line
Publication
PMID:27072656
Pathograph links
Increased Mutant GNAI3 Receptor Association measures Co-immunoprecipitation shows greater mutant association with EDNRA.
Limitations: Association abundance is not an equilibrium-affinity or residence-time measurement.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:27072656 SUPPORT PRIMARY RESULT In Vitro
"We found that all 5 ACS-associated Gαi3 mutants displayed increased binding (3- to 5-fold) to myc-tagged ETAR compared to Gαi3 WT (Fig. 5D)."
Co-immunoprecipitation measures increased receptor association, not a directly measured receptor residence time or equilibrium affinity.
Reduced EDNRA-Gq Signaling measures BRET measures reduced Gq activation after ET-1 stimulation.
Limitations: Heterologous expression does not establish the exact signaling stoichiometry in human arch cells.

Plcb4 Arg621His edited mouse founders
CRISPR/HDR founders show proximal mandibular dysplasia, reduced or absent mandibular processes and ectopic bony structures. Three knock-in-bearing embryos were identified among 69 collected. Wild-type/indel embryos and unmodified controls lacked overt skull changes in the reported comparison.
Species
Mouse
Genotype
One Plcb4 Arg621His/Arg621His and two Arg621His/indel founders
Background
C57BL/6NJ zygotes; F0 embryos examined at E18.5

Limitations: Small F0 sample and mixed edited genotypes limit extrapolation to dominant human inheritance.

Condylar, coronoid and angular processes are hypoplastic or absent.

Endothelin-pathway knockout mice
These knockout models demonstrate mandibular-to-maxillary homeosis and other craniofacial abnormalities. They establish developmental pathway requirements rather than reproducing the precise human alleles.
Species
Mouse
Genotype
Edn1-null, Ednra-null or Ece1-null models summarized in the developmental literature
Pathograph links
Disrupted Mandibular Neural Crest Identity perturbs Pathway-gene deletion changes mandibular positional identity.
Limitations: Different genes and null alleles are grouped only for the shared developmental result.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:24123988 SUPPORT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Model Organism
"Disruption of this signaling pathway in both mouse and zebrafish results in loss of identity of neural crest cells of the mandibular portion of the first pharyngeal arch and the subsequent repatterning of these cells, leading to homeosis of lower jaw structures into more maxillary-like structures."
Genetic developmental models support altered identity rather than a universal neural-crest migration deficit.
Null models can have cardiovascular defects and severe neonatal outcomes beyond typical human ACS. Partial allelic reduction and species-specific compensation complicate direct phenotype matching.

The review summarizes distinct developmental knockout models.

name: Auriculocondylar Syndrome
creation_date: '2026-05-10T15:02:55Z'
description: Auriculocondylar syndrome is a congenital craniofacial disorder characterized by variable mandibular and condylar hypoplasia, question-mark ears and associated oral or temporomandibular abnormalities. Severe presentations can compromise breathing and feeding. Dominant GNAI3 or PLCB4 variants, recessive PLCB4 or EDN1 alleles, and a reported HDAC9-TWIST1 regulatory duplication act through distinct molecular routes. Development, hearing and extra-craniofacial findings are variable; incomplete penetrance occurs.
category: Mendelian
parents:
- ear malformation
- craniofacial malformation syndrome
synonyms:
- auriculo-condylar syndrome
- question mark ear syndrome
- dysgnathia complex

  description: Recessive auriculocondylar syndrome has been reported for EDN1 variants and for biallelic PLCB4 loss-of-function lesions.

pathophysiology:
- name: Heterozygous GNAI3 Variants
  biological_scale: MOLECULAR
  description: Heterozygous pathogenic GNAI3 variants cluster in nucleotide-binding regions. De novo and inherited alleles occur, with variable expression and some clinically unaffected carriers. Functional conclusions below apply to the five tested missense variants rather than every subsequently reported allele.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:22560091
    reference_title: A human homeotic transformation resulting from mutations in PLCB4 and GNAI3 causes auriculocondylar syndrome.
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    quote_role: PRIMARY_RESULT
    snippet: In both kindreds, the p.Gly40Arg variant was inherited; in one case (S011) it was inherited from an affected mother (S012-P), and in the other (S008), from an unaffected father (S009-P), documenting incomplete penetrance.
    explanation: Two molecularly characterized families establish dominant inheritance with incomplete penetrance.

- name: Impaired GNAI3 GTP Binding
  biological_scale: MOLECULAR
  description: Purified GNAI3 S47R retains GDP binding but fails to bind GTP efficiently. G40R, G45V, T48N and N269Y also show impaired GTP-dependent protease protection after expression in HEK293T cells. This replaces the earlier structure-based gain-of-function prediction; it does not establish generalized loss of GDP binding.
  evidence:

- name: Reduced EDNRA-Gq Signaling
  biological_scale: MOLECULAR
  description: EDNRA preferentially activates Gq in the tested heterologous system. Disease-associated GNAI3 proteins suppress that response while leaving the weak Gi3 response largely unchanged under the same conditions. Normal GNAI3 is therefore not represented as an obligatory positive intermediate between EDNRA and PLCB4.

- name: PLCB4 Disease Alleles
  biological_scale: MOLECULAR
  description: PLCB4 disease alleles include dominant catalytic-domain missense variants and recessive biallelic loss-of-function lesions. Haploinsufficiency alone does not explain the tested dominant variants. The neonatal Ser643Phe report classified its variant as likely pathogenic and supplied structural prediction, not a catalytic assay.

    explanation: Summarizes previously established biallelic PLCB4 cases.

- name: Reduced EDNRA-Dependent Phospholipase C Activity
  biological_scale: MOLECULAR
  description: EDN1-stimulated DAG-sensor translocation and BRET are reduced by tested PLCB4 variants in HEK293T cells. Introducing Pro838Ala at the Gq-interaction interface in cis with Arg621His attenuates its dominant interference. These are cell-based pathway readouts, not purified-enzyme kinetics; substrate trapping and altered GAP activity remain untested explanations.

- name: EDN1 Disease Alleles
  biological_scale: MOLECULAR
  description: Biallelic EDN1 substitutions were identified in recessive ACS families. Other EDN1 alleles cause dominant isolated question-mark ears, and a later start-loss heterozygote had mild mandibular involvement. Phenotype and inheritance depend on the allele; measured residual ligand activity is not available for the original patient substitutions.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:24268655

    quote_role: PRIMARY_RESULT
    snippet: In family F1, the unaffected parents and child were heterozygous for the mutation, whereas the three affected siblings were homozygous, indicating that the allele encoding the p.Lys91Glu substitution behaves in a recessive manner.
    explanation: Segregation supports recessive inheritance of Lys91Glu in this family.

- name: Disrupted Mandibular Neural Crest Identity
  biological_scale: CELLULAR
  description: EDNRA-DLX disruption changes the positional identity of mandibular neural-crest-derived mesenchyme in animal models. Human skeletal morphology is consistent with partial mandibular-to-maxillary homeosis; it does not prove that every clinical feature arises through this transformation.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:24123988
    reference_title: 'Understanding the basis of auriculocondylar syndrome: Insights from human, mouse and zebrafish genetic studies.'
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: MODEL_ORGANISM
    quote_role: REVIEW_SYNTHESIS
    snippet: Disruption of this signaling pathway in both mouse and zebrafish results in loss of identity of neural crest cells of the mandibular portion of the first pharyngeal arch and the subsequent repatterning of these cells, leading to homeosis of lower jaw structures into more maxillary-like structures.
    explanation: Genetic developmental models support altered identity rather than a universal neural-crest migration deficit.
  conforms_to: pharyngeal_arch_patterning_serial_homology#Disrupted Pharyngeal-Arch Patterning and Neural-Crest Skeletogenesis

- name: HDAC9-TWIST1 Regulatory Duplication
  biological_scale: MOLECULAR
  description: A 430-kb tandem duplication within HDAC9 segregated with ACS in one Brazilian family. It includes candidate TWIST1 regulatory elements while leaving the TWIST1 coding region outside the duplication. Some short HDAC9 isoforms are wholly duplicated, whereas longer catalytic isoforms cross a breakpoint.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:34750192
    reference_title: 'New locus underlying auriculocondylar syndrome (ARCND): 430 kb duplication involving TWIST1 regulatory elements.'
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    quote_role: PRIMARY_RESULT
    snippet: The duplication spanned 430 302 bp (NC_000007.14:g.18437239_18867540dup) telomeric to TWIST1, covering most of the HDAC9 gene.

- name: GNAI3 pathogenic variants
  subtype: ARCND1
  gene_term:
    preferred_term: GNAI3
    term:
      id: hgnc:4387
      label: GNAI3
  relationship_type: CAUSATIVE
  association: Causative
  notes: Heterozygous GNAI3 nucleotide-binding-region variants can be inherited or de novo, with incomplete penetrance. Five tested missense proteins show defective activation and interference with EDNRA-Gq signaling. Later in-frame insertions and novel substitutions should not automatically inherit every experimentally established functional property.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:22560091
    reference_title: A human homeotic transformation resulting from mutations in PLCB4 and GNAI3 causes auriculocondylar syndrome.
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    quote_role: PRIMARY_RESULT
    snippet: In both kindreds, the p.Gly40Arg variant was inherited; in one case (S011) it was inherited from an affected mother (S012-P), and in the other (S008), from an unaffected father (S009-P), documenting incomplete penetrance.
    explanation: Two molecularly characterized families establish dominant inheritance with incomplete penetrance.
  - reference: PMID:27072656
    reference_title: Dominant-negative Gα subunits are a mechanism of dysregulated heterotrimeric G protein signaling in human disease.
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: IN_VITRO
    quote_role: PRIMARY_RESULT
    snippet: Together, these results demonstrate that Gαi3 S47R retains the capability to bind GDP but fails to bind GTP, rendering the G protein unable to switch into an active conformation.
    explanation: Purified S47R directly retains GDP but loses efficient GTP binding; other variants were assessed with cell-expressed limited-proteolysis assays.
- name: PLCB4 pathogenic variants
  subtype: ARCND2A
  gene_term:
    preferred_term: PLCB4
    term:
      id: hgnc:9059
      label: PLCB4
  relationship_type: CAUSATIVE
  association: Causative
  notes: Dominant catalytic-domain missense variants include recurrent Arg621 substitutions. Arg621His, Tyr623Cys, Glu358Val and Asp360Val were tested in cell signaling assays. The neonatal Ser643Phe variant was classified as likely pathogenic; its functional impairment was predicted rather than measured.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:35284927
    reference_title: Auriculocondylar syndrome 2 results from the dominant-negative action of PLCB4 variants.
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: IN_VITRO
    quote_role: PRIMARY_RESULT
    snippet: These results illustrate that PLCB4 p.Arg621His interferes with PLCB activity even when it is expressed at a sub-stoichiometric level relative to wild-type PLCB4, exemplifying a characteristic of a dominant-negative protein.
    explanation: DAG-sensor BRET detects dominant interference by Arg621His; three additional tested missense variants also attenuated the response.
- name: Biallelic PLCB4 disease alleles
  subtype: ARCND2B
  gene_term:
    preferred_term: PLCB4
    term:
      id: hgnc:9059
      label: PLCB4
  relationship_type: CAUSATIVE
  association: Causative
  notes: Recessive disease occurs with biallelic deletion, frameshift or splice alleles. Unaffected heterozygous deletion carriers distinguish this route from dominant missense interference. Small series include central apnea, developmental, gastrointestinal and genital findings, including macropenis, without establishing deterministic subtype frequencies.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:23315542
    reference_title: Heterogeneity of mutational mechanisms and modes of inheritance in auriculocondylar syndrome.
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    quote_role: PRIMARY_RESULT
    snippet: The consanguineous parents of the patient with a homozygous PLCB4 deletion each harboured the heterozygous deletion, but did not present the ACS phenotype, further suggesting that ACS is not caused by PLCB4 haploinsufficiency.
    explanation: Segregation demonstrates a recessive deletion mechanism in one family.

      id: hgnc:3176
      label: EDN1
  relationship_type: CAUSATIVE
  association: Causative
  notes: EDN1 alleles differ in inheritance and phenotype. Lys91Glu and Pro77His homozygotes have ACS; dominant Val64Asp and Tyr83* families initially showed isolated ear findings. The proposed reduction of mature ligand activity is based on sequence context and related experimental substitutions, not direct functional assays of these patient alleles.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:24268655
    reference_title: Mutations in endothelin 1 cause recessive auriculocondylar syndrome and dominant isolated question-mark ears.
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    quote_role: PRIMARY_RESULT
    snippet: In family F1, the unaffected parents and child were heterozygous for the mutation, whereas the three affected siblings were homozygous, indicating that the allele encoding the p.Lys91Glu substitution behaves in a recessive manner.
    explanation: Segregation supports recessive inheritance of Lys91Glu in this family.
  - reference: PMID:24268655
    reference_title: Mutations in endothelin 1 cause recessive auriculocondylar syndrome and dominant isolated question-mark ears.
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    quote_role: PRIMARY_RESULT
    snippet: The different mode of inheritance for the F1 and S1 mutant EDN1 alleles compared to the F2 and F3 alleles might be explained by differences in the amount of residual functional protein in each case.
    explanation: The authors explicitly propose residual functional ligand as an explanation; patient-allele processing was not directly measured.
    directness: INDIRECT
- name: TWIST1 regulatory duplication
  subtype: ARCND4
  gene_term:
    preferred_term: TWIST1
    term:
      id: hgnc:12428
      label: TWIST1
  relationship_type: CAUSATIVE
  association: Causative
  notes: A single-family noncoding regulatory-locus duplication excludes TWIST1 coding sequence and affects multiple HDAC9 isoforms and candidate regulatory elements. TWIST1 and HDAC9 upregulation in induced neural-crest cells is associated with the lesion, but TWIST1-specific rescue and in-vivo mediation have not been demonstrated.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:34750192
    reference_title: 'New locus underlying auriculocondylar syndrome (ARCND): 430 kb duplication involving TWIST1 regulatory elements.'
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    quote_role: PRIMARY_RESULT
    snippet: The duplication spanned 430 302 bp (NC_000007.14:g.18437239_18867540dup) telomeric to TWIST1, covering most of the HDAC9 gene.
    explanation: A segregating tandem duplication in one four-generation family defines the regulatory locus; TWIST1 coding sequence is not duplicated.
  - reference: PMID:34750192
    reference_title: 'New locus underlying auriculocondylar syndrome (ARCND): 430 kb duplication involving TWIST1 regulatory elements.'
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: IN_VITRO
    quote_role: PRIMARY_RESULT
    snippet: However, an increase in HDAC9 (3.15-fold, unpaired t-test p=0.009) and TWIST1 (2.03-fold, unpaired t-test p=0.03) mRNA was observed in the ARCND-iNCC (figure 2A, B) compared with controls (unpaired t-test).
    explanation: Three affected-donor iPSC-derived neural-crest cultures show increased transcripts relative to three controls; no isogenic correction isolates the causal mediator.
    directness: INDIRECT
diagnosis:
- name: Clinical and craniofacial imaging assessment
  description: Recognize the variable combination of question-mark ears, micrognathia and condylar or temporomandibular abnormalities. Examine oral structures, hearing and airway function. Imaging can characterize ramus, condyle, joint and temporal-bone anatomy where clinically indicated; absence of a complete triad does not exclude disease.
  evidence:
  - reference: url:https://pure.rug.nl/ws/files/217832848/Human_Mutation_2022_Vegas_Further_delineation_of_auriculocondylar_syndrome_based_on_14_novel_cases_and_reassessment.pdf

- name: Molecular and structural-variant testing
  description: Interpret targeted sequencing or exome/genome results together with phenotype and segregation. Established genes include GNAI3, PLCB4 and EDN1. Copy-number and regulatory-region analysis is relevant in unresolved cases because the reported HDAC9-TWIST1 duplication lies outside TWIST1 coding sequence. A VUS alone does not establish the diagnosis, and selected-case yields are not general diagnostic sensitivity.

  - reference: PMID:28328130

    supports: SUPPORT

    snippet: We found novel pathogenic variants in PLCB4 only in two of three index patients with typical Auriculocondylar syndrome.
    explanation: Two positive typical index cases in an 11-person mixed-phenotype series; not an unselected sensitivity estimate.

- name: Prenatal assessment
  description: Severe fetal micrognathia with polyhydramnios can prompt detailed craniofacial imaging and molecular evaluation. These findings are not specific for ACS, and question-mark ears may not be identified prenatally.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:34789173
    reference_title: 'Prenatal diagnosis of auriculocondylar syndrome with a novel missense variant of GNAI3: a case report.'
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    quote_role: PRIMARY_RESULT
    snippet: Severe micrognathia and mandibular hypoplasia accompanied by polyhydramnios are prenatal indicators of ACS.
    explanation: The reported fetus provides a possible diagnostic clue rather than a validated screening rule.
- name: Audiologic and sleep evaluation
  description: Audiograms help distinguish conductive, sensorineural and mixed or unclassified hearing loss. Sleep studies can distinguish obstructive and central components and inform follow-up after structural treatment.

progression:
- phase: Prenatal and neonatal presentation
  notes: Craniofacial malformations are congenital and severe mandibular hypoplasia can be recognized prenatally, sometimes with polyhydramnios. Severe neonates may need early airway and feeding intervention; question-mark ears can be absent.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:34789173
    reference_title: 'Prenatal diagnosis of auriculocondylar syndrome with a novel missense variant of GNAI3: a case report.'
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    quote_role: PRIMARY_RESULT
    snippet: Severe micrognathia and mandibular hypoplasia accompanied by polyhydramnios are prenatal indicators of ACS.
    explanation: The prenatal case supports early detectability, not diagnostic specificity.
- phase: Childhood and adult course
  notes: Joint restriction, sleep-disordered breathing, hearing, feeding, dental and articulation problems may require staged follow-up. Development is often normal but is not uniformly preserved. Structural treatment does not ensure resolution of central apnea. Adult corrective surgery has favorable reported outcomes in individual cases, not a comparative natural-history estimate.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:22560091

differential_diagnoses:
- name: Pierre Robin sequence-plus
  description: Pierre Robin sequence and related craniofacial disorders overlap through micrognathia, glossoptosis and palatal abnormalities. The ear pattern, condylar anatomy, other findings and molecular testing distinguish the spectrum; absence of question-mark ears alone does not exclude ACS.
  distinguishing_features:
  - Question mark ears
  - Mandibular condyle hypoplasia
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:28328130
    reference_title: Targeted molecular investigation in patients within the clinical spectrum of Auriculocondylar syndrome.
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: In order to address these questions, we searched for alterations in PLCB4, GNAI3, and EDN1 in patients with typical Auriculocondylar syndrome (n = 3), Pierre Robin sequence-plus (n = 3), micrognathia with additional craniofacial malformations (n = 4), or non-specific auricular dysplasia (n = 1), which could represent subtypes of Auriculocondylar syndrome.
    explanation: This supports Pierre Robin sequence-plus as part of the clinical differential or spectrum considered in testing.
    quote_role: PRIMARY_RESULT
- name: MEF2C-related neurodevelopmental disorder

- name: Oculo-auriculo-vertebral spectrum
  description: This craniofacial spectrum is a named source of diagnostic confusion. Assess the ear, mandibular-condylar and broader craniofacial pattern; the report identifies overlap but does not provide a validated diagnostic classifier.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:39014351
    reference_title: 'Novel GNAI3 mutation in a Chinese family with auriculocondylar syndrome and treatment of severe dentofacial deformities: a 5-year follow-up case report.'

    snippet: ARCND can be misdiagnosed as other diseases, including craniomaxillofacial deformities such as the oculo-auriculo-vertebral spectrum ... Treacher Collins syndrome ... mandibulofacial dysostosis ... Guion-Almeida type, and Meier-Gorlin syndrome
    explanation: The paper explicitly names this differential as background; it does not measure the sensitivity or specificity of distinguishing signs.
- name: Treacher Collins syndrome
  description: Treacher Collins syndrome is a named differential for the mandibulofacial presentation. Question-mark ear morphology and condylar findings can direct ARCND testing, but neither a single ear sign nor mandibular hypoplasia alone establishes the molecular diagnosis.

- name: Plcb4 Arg621His edited mouse founders
  species: Mouse
  background: C57BL/6NJ zygotes; F0 embryos examined at E18.5
  genotype: One Plcb4 Arg621His/Arg621His and two Arg621His/indel founders
  publication: PMID:35284927
  description: CRISPR/HDR founders show proximal mandibular dysplasia, reduced or absent mandibular processes and ectopic bony structures. Three knock-in-bearing embryos were identified among 69 collected. Wild-type/indel embryos and unmodified controls lacked overt skull changes in the reported comparison.
  notes: No wild-type/knock-in embryo was tested. These founder observations do not demonstrate the phenotype of a stable heterozygous line, adult function or therapeutic rescue. Tooth-like projections were morphologic observations rather than confirmed teeth.

- name: Endothelin-pathway knockout mice
  species: Mouse
  genotype: Edn1-null, Ednra-null or Ece1-null models summarized in the developmental literature
  description: These knockout models demonstrate mandibular-to-maxillary homeosis and other craniofacial abnormalities. They establish developmental pathway requirements rather than reproducing the precise human alleles.
  notes: Null models can have cardiovascular defects and severe neonatal outcomes beyond typical human ACS. Partial allelic reduction and species-specific compensation complicate direct phenotype matching.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:24123988
    reference_title: 'Understanding the basis of auriculocondylar syndrome: Insights from human, mouse and zebrafish genetic studies.'
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: MODEL_ORGANISM
    quote_role: REVIEW_SYNTHESIS
    snippet: mice that are homozygous null for Edn1, Ednra, endothelin converting enzyme 1 (Ece1) or Dlx5/Dlx6 display a homeotic transformation of the lower jaw into an upper jaw
    explanation: The review summarizes distinct developmental knockout models.
  modeled_mechanisms:
  - target: Disrupted Mandibular Neural Crest Identity
    relationship: PERTURBS
    limitations: Different genes and null alleles are grouped only for the shared developmental result.
    description: Pathway-gene deletion changes mandibular positional identity.
    evidence:
    - reference: PMID:24123988
      reference_title: 'Understanding the basis of auriculocondylar syndrome: Insights from human, mouse and zebrafish genetic studies.'
      supports: SUPPORT
      evidence_source: MODEL_ORGANISM
      quote_role: REVIEW_SYNTHESIS
      snippet: Disruption of this signaling pathway in both mouse and zebrafish results in loss of identity of neural crest cells of the mandibular portion of the first pharyngeal arch and the subsequent repatterning of these cells, leading to homeosis of lower jaw structures into more maxillary-like structures.
      explanation: Genetic developmental models support altered identity rather than a universal neural-crest migration deficit.
    model_scale: ORGANISM
- name: Zebrafish edn1 and plcb3 developmental mutants

  genotype: edn1 mutants and schmerle/plcb3 mutants summarized in the developmental literature
  description: Mutants show hypoplastic lower-jaw skeletal elements and fusion across arch joints. Genetic interaction supports a conserved endothelin-phospholipase pathway.
  notes: Zebrafish plcb3 is not the human PLCB4 gene, and species-specific compensation affects distal versus intermediate arch domains. These models do not establish a human treatment.

- name: GNAI3-variant expression in Xenopus embryos
  species: Xenopus laevis
  genotype: Injected mRNA encoding wild-type or ACS-associated GNAI3 variants
  description: Expression of G40R, G45V, S47R, T48N or N269Y increases a gastrulation-associated gravitational-inversion phenotype relative to wild-type GNAI3 expression at the tested doses.

datasets:
- accession: dbgap:phs000437
  title: 'Next Generation Mendelian Genetics: Auriculocondylar Syndrome (ACS)'
  description: This project is part of an ongoing project to identify the molecular genetic basis of rare craniofacial disorders. This specific project involved the collection of DNA samples from four probands with severe manifestations of auriculocondylar syndrome and their parents. Detailed clinical phenotypic data is available on each proband. The sole purpose of this research is to identify the molecular cause(s) of auriculochondylar syndrome.
  notes: The dbGaP accession and disease-specific title were verified against the live repository on 2026-09-21. The project describes four severe ACS probands and their parents; it is a gene-discovery collection, not a representative phenotype-frequency cohort.

  description: Recombinant wild-type and S47R GNAI3 were compared by nucleotide-binding, GTPase, HPLC and limited-proteolysis assays. S47R retains GDP but has profoundly impaired GTP binding and activation.

  experimental_model_type: CELL_LINE
  description: Five ACS-associated GNAI3 variants were studied in transfected HEK293T cells using protease protection, receptor co-immunoprecipitation and BRET. ET-1-induced Gq activation was reduced at an 8:1 Gq-to-Gi3 expression ratio; the weak Gi3 response was not comparably inhibited.

  notes: DAG-sensor and SRE readouts are not purified-enzyme kinetics, a direct ERK-phosphorylation assay or fetal DLX expression. Proposed PIP2 trapping and altered GAP function were not directly tested. The synthetic interface mutation is not a clinical therapy.
  publication: PMID:35284927
  cell_source: Transfected HEK293T cells for DAG assays; HEK293 cells for SRE reporter

discussions:
- discussion_id: arcnd_regulatory_mediation
  kind: KNOWLEDGE_GAP
  attaches_to:
  - pathophysiology#HDAC9-TWIST1 Regulatory Duplication
  - pathophysiology#Increased Neural Crest TWIST1 Expression
  prompt: Which regulatory and cellular changes mediate the HDAC9-region duplication phenotype?
  rationale: Segregation, nonpatient Capture-C and patient-cell expression findings support a regulatory locus. They do not isolate TWIST1 from HDAC9 or establish that the scratch and osteogenic readouts cause the human jaw phenotype. No serial TWIST1-to-migration-to-osteogenesis causal chain is asserted.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:34750192
    reference_title: 'New locus underlying auriculocondylar syndrome (ARCND): 430 kb duplication involving TWIST1 regulatory elements.'
